Pseudo:
```
def softmax_weights(C, beta):
    C = C - C.max()
    e = np.exp(beta*C)
    p=e/e.sum()
    return p
def grad_logsumexp(a, beta):
    n=len(a); C=conv(a,a)
    p = softmax_weights(C,beta)
    R = sliding_window_sum(p,n)
    r = p[::-1]
    c = fftconvolve(r,a, mode='full')[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
    G = c + a*R
    return G, C
```

Then gradient of logsumexp = beta*G.

```

def fast_score(a):
    n=len(a)

    if s<1e-8: return inf
    a=a/s
    C=convolution_fft(a,a)[:2n-1]

```

```
def _fft_conv_real(a,b):
    n=len(a)+len(b)-1
    N=1
    while N<n: N*=2
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N); fb=np.fft.rfft(b,N)
    return np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,N)[:n]
```
For correlation c: use N=next_pow2(2*n), pad a,p.
```
def _c_deriv(a,p):
    n=len(a); N=1; while N<2*n: N*=2
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N); fp=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    corr=np.fft.irfft(fa*np.conj(fp), N)
    idx=(-np.arange(n)) % N
    return corr[idx]
```

Implement `compute_C_and_grad(a, beta)`:
```
def _conv_self_C(a, N):
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    C=np.fft.irfft(A*A,N).real[:2*len(a)-1]
    C[C<0]=0
    return C
```

Let's focus on a simple but effective algorithm:
- Use random restarts with "multiplicative gradient descent" on actual score (smoothed max) for a few hundred iterations, starting from a set of promising shapes.
- Include a special "peak redistribution" operation: identify top peaks in C; for each peak k, the coefficient is sum of products along anti-diagonal i+j=k. We can reduce peak by moving mass from indices involved in that anti-diagonal to indices involved in under peak lags. A greedy move: Choose peak k, and low lag l. Find pair (i,j) with i+j=k high product and pair (u,v) with u+v=l low product; transfer δ from a_i or a_j to a_u/v? Could maintain nonneg and sum. This is like reflow. Maybe powerful.

Specifically, C_k = ∑_{i} a_i a_{k-i}. If peak at k, decrease a_i for some i in that diagonal. To compensate, increase a_m that contributes to low lags. Direct local search over index choices could find improvements. O(n) per move. Could run many.

```
def log_adam_restart(a, time_budget, beta0, betamax, lr=0.02, b1=0.9,b2=0.999, noise_every=100, noise_amp=0.1):
    n=len(a); a=a/sum; x=np.log(a+eps)
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n)
    best_a=a; best_score=fast_score(a)
    beta=beta0; it=0
    while time<deadline:
       C = self_conv(a)
       Cmax=C.max()
       p = softmax(beta*(C-Cmax))
       G = grad_C(a,p)
       H = a*G
       H -= H.mean()
       # Optional: regularize to keep positivity? Not needed.
       m = b1*m + (1-b1)*H
       v = b2*v + (1-b2)*H*H
       mhat=m/(1-b1**(it+1)); vhat=v/(1-b2**(it+1))
       step = lr * mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-8)
       x += step  # because maximize? We want minimize score; gradient points increase G, so subtract: x -= step. Wait H=grad of smooth max wrt x. So x_new = x - lr*... 
```

`self_convolve(a)`:
```
N = next_power_of_two(2*n)  # use one N
A = np.fft.rfft(a,N)
C = np.fft.irfft(A*A,N)
C = C[:2*n-1]
C[:]=np.maximum(C,0)
```
For n=1: N=2, C length1.

`gradient_smooth(a,p)`:
```
n=len(a); N=next_power_of_two(2*n)
# p length 2n-1; pad to N
fp = np.fft.rfft(p,N)
fa = np.fft.rfft(a,N)
corr = np.fft.irfft(fa*np.conj(fp), N)
idx = (-np.arange(n)) % N
c = corr[idx]
cdf = np.empty(2*n); cdf[0]=0; cdf[1:] = np.cumsum(p)  # length 2n, since p length 2n-1 -> cdf len 2n
R = cdf[n:2*n] - cdf[:n]
G = c + a*R
return G
```
Let's test for n=3 with correlation formula via N=8? p length5. Correlation output corr[t]=∑a_s p_{s-t mod N}. Desired c[j]=corr[(N-j)%N]. Good.
We should ensure p sum=1; due FFT small imaginary, real.

`optimize_log_adam(a, deadline, beta0, beta1, lr, ...)`
Detailed:
```
def optimize_log_adam(a, deadline, beta0=5, beta1=100, lr=0.02, ...): # beta in units of n maybe
    n=len(a); a=np.asarray(a,float).copy(); a=np.clip(a,0); s=a.sum(); if s<... return best
    a/=s
    if a.max()==0: return
    x=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300))
    # Maybe x = x - x.mean()
    best=a.copy(); best_score=fast_score(a)
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n)
    beta=beta0 * n
    beta_max=beta1 * n
    lr_current=lr
    no_improve=0
    it=0
    last_report=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        C=self_conv(a)
        score=2*n*C.max()
        if score < best_score - 1e-12:
             best_score=score; best=a.copy(); no_improve=0
             # maybe adapt lr up
        else:
             no_improve +=1
        Cmax=C.max()
        p=softmax(C,beta)
        G=grad(a,p)
        H=a*G
        H-=H.mean()
        # If H norm tiny, add noise
        m=0.9*m+0.1*H
        v=0.999*v+0.001*H*H
        bc1=1-0.9**it; bc2=1-0.999**it
        m_hat=m/bc1; v_hat=v/bc2
        denom=np.sqrt(v_hat)+1e-8
        # step normalization: lr_current * m_hat / denom
        step=lr_current * m_hat / denom
        x -= step
        # clip x range
        x = np.clip(x, -30, 5)
        a=np.exp(x)
        s=a.sum()
        if not finite or s<=0: fallback a=best; break
        a/=s
        # optional: if too many tiny, no
        it+=1
        beta = min(beta_max, beta*1.005) # or based on it
        # lr adapt
        if no_improve > 200:
             lr_current=max(0.002, lr_current*0.8)
             # random noise
             if it % 50 ==0:
                  noise=np.random.normal(0,0.05,n)
                  a *= np.exp(noise)
                  a/=a.sum()
                  # maybe reset momentum
        elif no_improve < 50:
             lr_current=min(0.1, lr_current*1.02)
        if time - last_report >30: print(...)
    return best,best_score
```

```
    deadline = start + budget_s - 10
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    n = choose_n()
```

Candidate generation code:
```
def make_base(n, shift=0.5):
    i=np.arange(n,dtype=float)+shift
    return 1/np.sqrt(i)
def norm_pos(a):
    a=np.clip(a,0,None); s=a.sum(); if s<=0: return None; return a/s
```

Let's write helper functions carefully.

`next_power_of_two(m)`:
```
def next_power_of_two(m):
    n=1
    while n < m:
        n <<= 1
    return n
```

`safe_normalize(a)`:
```
def safe_normalize(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim !=1 or a.size==0: return None
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,np.inf)
    if not np.isfinite(a).all(): return None
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-12: return None
    return a/s
```

`self_convolve(a, N=None)`:
```
n=len(a)
if N is None: N=next_power_of_two(2*n)
A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
C=np.fft.irfft(A*A,N)
L=2*n-1
C=C[:L]
C[C<0]=0
return C
```
Maybe due to numerical tiny negative, max unaffected.

`gradient_smooth(a,p,N=None)`:
```
n=len(a)
if N is None: N=next_power_of_two(2*n)
A=np.fft.rfft(a,N); P=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
corr=np.fft.irfft(A*np.conj(P),N).real
idx=(-np.arange(n)) % N
c=corr[idx]
# sliding sum
cdf=np.empty(2*n, dtype=float)
cdf[0]=0.0
cdf[1:]=np.cumsum(p)
R=cdf[n:2*n] - cdf[:n]
return c + a*R
```
Check cdf length: p length 2n-1; cumulative length 2n-1; assign to cdf[1:] length 2n-1, total 2n. Good.
If p has sum not exactly1 due numerical; ok.

```
if s<=0: p=zeros; p[argmax]=1; else p=e/s
return p
```

```
def log_adam_optimize(a, deadline, beta_scale_start=2.0, beta_scale_end=50.0, lr=0.01, b1=0.9, b2=0.999):
    a=safe_normalize(a)
    if a is None: return a, np.inf
    n=len(a)
    N=next_power_of_two(2*n)
    beta_start = max(1.0, beta_scale_start*n)
    beta_end = max(beta_start, beta_scale_end*n)
    x=np.log(a)
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n)
    best=a.copy(); best_score=fast_score(a,N)
    lr_cur=lr
    beta=beta_start
    it=0
    last_best=best_score
    stall=0
    next_noise=100
    report_last=0.0
    eps=1e-12
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        C=self_convolve(a,N)
        score=float(2*n*C.max())
        if score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score=score; best=a.copy(); stall=0
        else:
            stall += 1
        if score == np.inf or not np.isfinite(score):
            a=best.copy(); break
        # weights
        Cmax=float(C.max())
        p=softmax_weights(C,beta)
        G=gradient_smooth(a,p,N)
        H=a*G
        H -= H.mean()
        # small noise to avoid zero
        hnorm=float(np.linalg.norm(H))
        if hnorm < 1e-14:
            H=np.sin(np.linspace(0,100,n)) # arbitrary
            H -= H.mean()
        m = b1*m + (1-b1)*H
        v = b2*v + (1-b2)*(H*H)
        bc1=1.0 - b1**(it+1)
        bc2=1.0 - b2**(it+1)
        m_hat=m/bc1
        v_hat=v/bc2
        step = lr_cur * m_hat / (np.sqrt(v_hat)+1e-8)
        # limit step magnitude to avoid blowup
        max_step = 0.5 # log domain
        step = np.clip(step, -max_step, max_step)
        x = x - step
        # Clip x to keep a in sane range relative to sum? Since after normalize, use max shift
        x = x - x.max()
        x = np.clip(x, -50.0, 1.0)  # since max 0, min -50

        s=a_new.sum()
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
            a = best.copy()
            x = np.log(a)
            m[:] = 0; v[:] = 0
        else:
            a_new = a_new / s
            # If too sparse? keep
            a = a_new
            x = np.log(a)
        # adapt beta/lr
        beta = min(beta_end, beta*1.002)
        if stall > 500:
            lr_cur = max(0.002, lr_cur*0.7)

            if stall > next_noise:
```

```
def mirror_optimize(a, deadline, eta=0.05):
   best_score=fast_score(a)
   cur_score=best_score
   eta_cur=eta
   stall=0
   while time<deadline:
      C=self_convolve(a); score=...; if score<best...
      p=softmax(C,beta)
      G=grad; H=a*G; H-=H.mean()
      scale=float(np.max(np.abs(H))) +1e-12
      steps = eta_cur * H/scale
      steps=np.clip(steps, -5,5)
      a_new=a*np.exp(-steps)  # since H gradient in log
      a_new=safe_normalize(a_new)
      if a_new is None: a=best; break

          stall+=1

              a=best*exp(noise) normalize; cur_score=...
              eta_cur=0.05
```

```
def generate_initial_candidates(n, rng, max_cands=300):
    cands=[]
    t=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    i=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
    shifts=[0.1,0.25,0.5,1.0]
    base_list=[1/np.sqrt(i+s) for s in shifts]
    # include base alone
    # two-step: x_list, u_list
    for x in np.linspace(0.4,0.8,9):
       for u in np.linspace(0.35,0.65,9):
           a1=u/(n*x); a2=(1-u)/(n*(1-x))
           a=np.concatenate([np.full(int(n*x),a1), np.full(n-int(n*x),a2)])
           cands.append(a)
    # random smooth positive:
    for _ in range(20):
       a = np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.5,n) + (rng.uniform(-1,1))*t + (rng.uniform(-1,1))*t**2)
       # smooth with cumsum? no
       cands.append(a)
    # modulations of power
    for base in base_list:
       for gamma in [0.2,0.5,1,1.5,2]:
          for sign in [-1,1]:
             for amp in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3]:
                 a=base*(1+sign*amp*t**gamma)
                 cands.append(a)
                 a2=base*(1+sign*amp*(1-t)**gamma)
                 cands.append(a2)
       for freq in [0.5,1,2]:
          for phase in [0, np.pi/2, np.pi]:
             for amp in [0.1,0.2,0.3]:
                 a=base*(1+amp*np.sin(np.pi*freq*t+phase))
                 cands.append(a)
                 a=base*(1+amp*np.cos(np.pi*freq*t+phase))
                 cands.append(a)
    # include base height_sequence_1 normalized maybe
```

Pseudo:
```
def mirror_optimize(a, N, n, deadline, eta=0.05):
    a=safe_normalize(a)
    best_score=fast_score_normalized(a,N,n)
    best=a.copy()
    cur_score=best_score
    eta_cur=eta
    beta=max(1.0, n)
    stall=0
    it=0
    last_report=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       C=self_convolve(a,N)
       cur_score=float(2*n*C.max())
       if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
           best_score=cur_score; best=a.copy(); stall=0
       else:
           stall += 1
       Cmax=C.max()
       p=softmax_weights(C,beta)
       G=gradient_smooth(a,p,N)
       H=a*G
       H-=H.mean()
       scale=float(np.max(np.abs(H)))
       if scale <= 1e-14:
           # random direction
           H=_RNG.normal(0,1,n); H-=H.mean(); scale=1
       step=eta_cur*H/scale
       step=np.clip(step,-4,4)
       a_new=a*np.exp(-step)
       a_new=safe_normalize(a_new)
       if a_new is None:
           a=best.copy(); break
       C_new=self_convolve(a_new,N)
       score_new=float(2*n*C_new.max())
       if score_new < cur_score - 1e-14:
           a=a_new; cur_score=score_new
           eta_cur=min(0.2, eta_cur*1.02)
           stall=0
       else:
           eta_cur=max(0.002, eta_cur*0.75)
           stall += 1
           if stall > 300:
               # try several random perturbations of best
               improved=False
               for _ in range(5):
                   amp=0.05+0.1*_RNG.random()
                   cand=best*np.exp(_RNG.normal(0,amp,n))
                   cand=safe_normalize(cand)
                   if cand is None: continue
                   s2=float(2*n*self_convolve(cand,N).max())
                   if s2 < cur_score:
                       a=cand; cur_score=s2; improved=True; break
                       # also if s2 < best_score update? yes
               if improved:
                   if cur_score < best_score:
                       best_score=cur_score; best=a.copy()
                   stall=0; eta_cur=0.05
               else:
                   # block perturb best
                   cand=best.copy()
                   width=max(1,n//100)
                   for __ in range(5):
                       start=int(_RNG.integers(0,n-width+1))
                       cand[start:start+width]*=float(np.exp(_RNG.normal(0,0.1)))
                       cand=safe_normalize(cand)
                       if cand is None: break
                       s2=float(2*n*self_convolve(cand,N).max())
                       if s2 < cur_score:
                           a=cand; cur_score=s2; improved=True; break
                   if improved:
                       if cur_score < best_score: best...; stall=0; eta_cur=0.05
                   else:
                       stall=0; # avoid infinite reset? maybe continue
       beta = min(100*n, beta*1.001)
       it+=1
       if it % 200 ==0 and verbose: print...
    return best,best_score
```
There is a subtle issue: If score_new not < cur_score, we leave a as old (not a_new). We discarded a_new. But eta decreases; next step from same a with smaller eta. Good. However if eta becomes very small and gradient still not improving (local min), stall triggers random perturb.
If we accept a_new, cur_score updated but we have already computed best_score maybe earlier using C of old a. We update if score_new < best_score after accept.

But if score_new improves but not best? We accept; best remains lower? Actually if score_new < cur_score (old), and old cur_score may be >best? If best was lower than current due previous bad? But if we don't accept a_new, current unchanged. So cur_score=best_score always, unless random reset to a worse candidate? We reset to perturbation if s2<cur_score, so still improvement. So current is best. Good.

However if gradient step from current with eta too large jumps to a worse score, we reject; try smaller next. This is line-search one-dimensional. If no scale improves (eta already small), stall. Good.

`choose_n`:
```
def choose_sequence_length(base):
    if base is not None and base.size>=256 and base.size<=8000:
```

Mirror step uses H = a*G. But our objective in normalized a: F = 2n Cmax / S^2. If we normalize a after each step, the gradient of F at a with sum=1 maybe G - (2 Cmax/S) a? The `a*G` ignores scale term. The scale term is constant times a; in log update, it corresponds to uniform shift in x, which we remove by H-=H.mean? Wait scale term in a gradient is proportional to a, not constant. In log gradient, a*(c a) = c a^2, not uniform. Scale invariance direction in a is a, but gradient component along a may not be simply mean. Let's derive.
F(a)=M(a)/S^2 where M homogeneous degree2. ∇_a F = (∇M - 2 M/S a)/S. The second term is proportional to a. The log gradient = a ∇_a F = a∇M - 2M/S a^2. The second term is not uniform in log; it penalizes increasing coordinates with larger a more. But if we normalize a after each update, do we need exact gradient? We can instead optimize a directly on simplex using ∇_a F. Mirror step in a maybe `a_new = a - η * ∇_a F`? But scale invariance means adding c a after normalization may not be irrelevant? Multiplying a by (1+c) (uniform scaling) after normalization no change; subtracting c a changes shape? If a_i all multiplied by different? c a is proportional to a, which in log is c (constant) across coordinates? Wait if a_i are current values, updating `a_i <- a_i - c a_i = (1-c)a_i` is uniform scaling in a (same relative factor for all coordinates), which after normalization leaves a unchanged. In log, log a_i <- log a_i + log(1-c) for all i, a uniform shift. So the component proportional to a in a-gradient corresponds to uniform shift in log, and `H -= H.mean` does not remove it if a varies? In log gradient, component from c a is a_i * c a_i? No, if ∇_a F has component c a, log gradient is a_i * c a_i = c a_i^2, not constant. But moving `x_i <- x_i - ε c a_i^2` is not uniform; it changes shape. However the corresponding a update for gradient descent in a: a_i <- a_i - η c a_i, which is uniform relative decrease, not a_i^2. Mirror descent uses log gradient, not Euclidean gradient. For mirror geometry with K=a log a, the natural direction for ∇_a proportional to a is a exp(-η c)=constant scaling? Let's see: Mirror update `a_new = a * exp(-η ∇_a F)` uses a gradient, not log gradient. If ∇_a F has component c a, exp(-η c a) is not constant if a varies? Actually a*exp(-η c a) not uniform. The natural mirror update for multiplicative is often `a_new = a * exp(-η ∇_a F)` where ∇_a is derived from objective in a. The scale term c a would produce exp(-η c a_i), not uniform. But in optimization over ratios, the exact scale term might matter; it's not simply removable for multiplicative updates. However we can avoid by reparameterizing ratios and using objective F(a)/scale invariant; we can compute exact gradient wrt log a after quotienting by sum. Let's derive exact log gradient for F with normalization.
Let B = a/S (normalized). F=2n M(B) (score shape). Let g = ∇_B M (or smooth). We want gradient wrt x where B=exp(x)/S. As earlier, dF/dx_i = a_i? Let's derive with B directly.
Let l = log a. Normalize: B_i = e^{l_i}/Z, Z=∑ e^{l_i}. dB_i/dl_j = δ_ij B_i - B_i B_j.
F(l)=F(B). Gradient wrt l: ∑_i g_i (δ_ij B_i - B_i B_j) = g_j B_j - B_j ∑ g_i B_i = B_j (g_j - \bar g), where \bar g=∑g_i B_i. This removes the scale term exactly! Here g = ∇_B M (gradient of M wrt normalized B). Our G from C for a? If a normalized B, C(B), G=∇_B M (for M=smooth max of C). So exact log gradient H_i = B_i (G_i - weighted_mean(G,B)). We were using H=a*G - mean(H). That's different. The correct H in B normalized: `H = a * (G - dot(G,a))` because sum a=1, weighted mean = dot(G,a). This is rank-1 correction proportional to a. In terms of a, H = a*G - a*dot(G,a). This projects G onto weighted orthogonal complement. Our `H=a*G; H-=H.mean` is wrong. Use weighted projection: `g_mean = float(np.dot(G, a))`; `H = a * (G - g_mean)`. This is a*G - a*g_mean. Good. This removes scale direction exactly and is appropriate for log updates. The scale term is c*a in G? Actually if G has component c*a, dot(G,a)=c, G-c*a projected to zero? a*(c*a - c*a)=0. Good.

So in mirror/Adam, use `g_mean = np.dot(G,a); H = a*(G - g_mean)`.
Normalize H? We can scale by maxabs. Good. This is crucial.

Let's write `mirror_optimize` robustly:
```
def mirror_optimize(a, N, n, deadline, eta=0.05, max_its=None):
    a=safe_normalize(a)
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score_norm(a,N,n)
    cur_score=best_score
    eta_cur=eta
    beta=max(1.0, n)
    stall=0; it=0; last_reset=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        C=self_convolve(a,N); cur_score=score from C
        if cur_score < best_score:
            best_score=cur_score; best=a.copy()
        Cmax=C.max(); p=softmax; G; gmean=dot(G,a); H=a*(G-gmean)
        scale=maxabs(H)+1e-14
        if scale small: H random; ...
        step=eta_cur*H/scale; clip
        a_new=a*exp(-step); normalize
        score_new = ...
        anneal = max(0.001, 0.01*np.exp(-it/3000.0))
        if score_new <= cur_score + anneal:
            if score_new < cur_score:
                eta_cur=min(0.25, eta_cur*1.05)
                stall=0
            else:
                # accepted worse, keep eta or reduce slightly
                if eta_cur > 0.02: eta_cur*=0.9
                stall +=1
            a=a_new; cur_score=score_new
            if score_new < best_score: best_score=score_new; best=a.copy()
        else:
            eta_cur=max(0.001, eta_cur*0.7)
            stall+=1
        beta=min(200*n, beta*1.001)
        it+=1
        if cur_score > best_score + 0.05 or (stall>500 and it>LastReset+100):
            # reset to best with noise
            amp=0.03 + 0.07*_RNG.random()
            cand=best*np.exp(_RNG.normal(0,amp,n)); cand=safe_normalize(cand)
            if cand is not None:
               s=score_norm(cand,N,n)
               a=cand; cur_score=s; eta_cur=0.05; stall=0; last_reset=it
            else: a=best.copy(); cur_score=best_score
        if it%500==0: print(...)
    return best,best_score
```
If accepted worse, cur_score may be >best, but `best` remains. Reset condition if too worse.

Let's derive continuous perturbation more. For f0, operation T(q)(t)=∫_0^t q(i)/√{i(t-i)} di for t∈[0,1]. We want T(q)(t)≤0 for t∈[0,1] and weighted mean ∫ q/√i di=0, with q > -1 (so f≥0) if f=f0(1+ε q). Since T is linear and positive for positive q. Is there q with zero weighted mean and T(q)<0? This is like q in cone of functions whose fractional integral is negative. Since q must positive somewhere. Suppose q is a positive bump near t=1 and negative near t=0. For t< bump location, T only sees negative near0 -> negative. For t beyond bump, positive contributes; can T remain negative if bump amplitude and location chosen? At t=1, T(1)=∫0^1 q(i)/√{i(1-i)} di. Weight w1. Weight mean constraint ∫q/√i di. Let choose q = δ at position x (positive) - αδ at y (negative) to satisfy weighted mean. As t passes x, positive. Want T(1) negative: α/√{y(1-y)} > 1/√{x(1-x)}, so α > √{y(1-y)/x(1-x)}. Mean constraint: -α/√y + 1/√x=0 => α=√{x/y}. Combine: √{x/y} > √{y(1-y)/x(1-x)} => x/y > y(1-y)/(x(1-x)) => x(1-x) > y^2(1-y). Since 1-y <1, condition x(1-x) > y^2. If choose y small, need x(1-x)>small. Possible. For y=0.01, x=0.5 works: 0.25>1e-4. Then T(1) negative. But for t between y and x, negative contributes, T negative. For t just after x, positive contributes; may become positive if too strong. Need check. With delta, discontinuous but approximates spikes. This suggests a q with negative spike near 0 and positive bump at 0.5 can make T<0 all t if amplitude right. f=f0(1+εq) nonnegative (negative spike reduces f0 near endpoint, positive middle increases). This may lower plateau. So possible.

```
def choose_n_and_base(base):
    if base is not None and base.size >= 512 and base.size <= 8000:
         return int(base.size), base
    n=4096
    if base is not None:
        xb=np.linspace(0,1,n)
        tb=np.linspace(0,1,base.size)
        rb=np.interp(xb,tb,base)
        rb=np.clip(rb,0,None)
        if rb.sum()>0: return n, rb
    return n, None
```

Let's ensure `height_sequence_1` if defined as list of ints length 4498; `prepare_global_base` clips. In `choose_n_and_base`, if base valid and size 4498, returns b (raw). In build_candidate_pool, base raw values maybe all 1; add_candidate normalizes. Good.

If base has sum low after clip? safe normalize in add. choose uses base if finite but not normalized; okay.

One more potential improvement: Use final best power sequence if actual evaluate of optimized gives worse than due FFT errors? Actual top evaluation will choose best. Good.

Now final code.
</think>

```python
"""
Search for low-peak self-convolution sequences.

The method starts from a one-sided inverse-square-root shape and a broad set of
smoothly modulated variants, then uses projected mirror-descent with line search
on a smoothed maximum of the full linear self-convolution.  The score is
scale-invariant, so all sequences are kept normalized to sum 1 internally.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

_RNG = np.random.default_rng(987654321)


def next_power_of_two(m):
    n = 1
    while n < m:
        n <<= 1
    return n


def prepare_global_base():
    try:
        b = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return None
    if b.ndim != 1 or b.size == 0:
        return None
    b = np.clip(b, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if not np.isfinite(b).all():
        return None
    s = float(b.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return None
    return b


def choose_n_and_base(base):
    if base is not None and base.size >= 512 and base.size <= 8000:
        return int(base.size), base

    n = 4096
    if base is not None and base.size > 0:
        tb = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, int(base.size))
        xb = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
        rb = np.interp(xb, tb, base)
        rb = np.clip(rb, 0.0, None)
        if np.isfinite(rb).all() and rb.sum() > 1e-12:
            return n, rb
    return n, None


def safe_normalize(raw):
    a = np.asarray(raw, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1 or a.size == 0:
        return None
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if not np.isfinite(a).all():
        return None
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return None
    return a / s


def self_convolve(a, N=None):
    n = int(a.size)
    if N is None:
        N = next_power_of_two(2 * n)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, N)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    c = c[:L]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def fast_score_normalized(a, N, n):
    c = self_convolve(a, N)
    return float(2.0 * int(n) * float(c.max()))


def score_raw_candidate(raw, n, N):
    a = safe_normalize(raw)
    if a is None:
        return None
    s = fast_score_normalized(a, N, n)
    if not np.isfinite(s):
        return None
    return (s, id(a), a)


def add_candidate(top, raw, n, N):
    res = score_raw_candidate(raw, n, N)
    if res is None:
        return
    top.append(res)
    if len(top) > 180:
        top.sort()
        del top[180:]


def build_candidate_pool(n, N, base, limit=14):
    top = []
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)

    # One-sided square-root singular shape.  This gives an almost perfectly flat
    # convolution on the first half of the lags and is a strong all-round start.
    for sh in (0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0):
        add_candidate(top, 1.0 / np.sqrt(i + sh), n, N)

    # Two-block ramp shapes.
    for xf in np.linspace(0.35, 0.80, 10):
        m = int(round(n * xf))
        if m <= 0 or m >= n:
            continue
        for h in (0.3, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0, 5.0):
            raw = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
            raw[:m] = 1.0
            raw[m:] = h
            add_candidate(top, raw, n, N)

    # Smooth positive random shapes.
    for _ in range(60):
        c0 = _RNG.normal(0.0, 0.4)
        c1 = _RNG.normal(0.0, 0.7)
        c2 = _RNG.normal(0.0, 0.5)
        f = _RNG.uniform(0.5, 2.5)
        raw = np.exp(c0 + c1 * t + c2 * t * t + _RNG.normal(0.0, 0.25) * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * f * t))
        add_candidate(top, raw, n, N)

    # Modulated square-root shapes.
    for sh in (0.25, 0.5, 1.0):
        base_raw = 1.0 / np.sqrt(i + sh)
        add_candidate(top, base_raw, n, N)

        for g in (0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0):
            for sgn in (-1.0, 1.0):
                for amp in (0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 0.5):
                    tt = t ** g
                    add_candidate(top, base_raw * (1.0 + sgn * amp * tt), n, N)
                    add_candidate(top, base_raw * (1.0 + sgn * amp * (1.0 - t) ** g), n, N)

        hump = np.sin(np.pi * t) ** 2
        for amp in (0.1, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0):
            add_candidate(top, base_raw * (1.0 + amp * hump), n, N)
            add_candidate(top, base_raw * (1.0 + amp * (hump - 0.5)), n, N)
            add_candidate(top, base_raw * (1.0 - 0.5 * amp * hump), n, N)
            add_candidate(top, base_raw * np.exp(amp * (hump - 0.5)), n, N)

        tr = t * (1.0 - t)
        for amp in (0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0):
            add_candidate(top, base_raw * np.exp(amp * (tr - 0.25)), n, N)
            add_candidate(top, base_raw * (1.0 + amp * (tr - 0.25)), n, N)

        root_t = np.sqrt(np.maximum(t, 1e-8))
        root_t = root_t - root_t.mean()
        for amp in (0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
            add_candidate(top, base_raw * np.exp(amp * root_t), n, N)

        for freq in (1.0, 2.0, 3.0):
            for phase in (0.0, 0.5 * np.pi, np.pi):
                for amp in (0.1, 0.2, 0.4):
                    w = freq * np.pi * t + phase
                    add_candidate(top, base_raw * (1.0 + amp * np.sin(w)), n, N)
                    add_candidate(top, base_raw * (1.0 + amp * np.cos(w)), n, N)

        # Low-degree positive multiplier polynomial/Hermite-like perturbations.
        for _ in range(40):
            coef = _RNG.normal(0.0, 0.25, 6)
            mult = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
            for k in range(6):
                if coef[k] != 0.0:
                    mult = mult + coef[k] * (t ** k)
            add_candidate(top, base_raw * mult, n, N)

    # Use the provided construction if present, plus simple blends with the
    # square-root shape.
    if base is not None and base.size == n:
        add_candidate(top, base, n, N)
        pow_shape = 1.0 / np.sqrt(i + 0.5)
        add_candidate(top, base * pow_shape, n, N)
        add_candidate(top, base + pow_shape, n, N)
        add_candidate(top, base / (pow_shape + 0.05), n, N)

    top.sort()
    return top[:limit]


def softmax_weights(C, beta):
    m = float(C.max())
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        p = np.ones(C.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(C.size)
        return p
    z = beta * (C - m)
    z = np.clip(z, -700.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        p = np.zeros(C.size, dtype=np.float64)
        p[int(np.argmax(C))] = 1.0
        return p
    return e / s


def gradient_smooth(a, p, N=None):
    n = int(a.size)
    if N is None:
        N = next_power_of_two(2 * n)

    A = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    corr = np.fft.irfft(A * np.conj(P), N)
    idx = (-np.arange(n)) % N
    cpart = corr[idx]

    cdf = np.empty(2 * n, dtype=np.float64)
    cdf[0] = 0.0
    cdf[1:] = np.cumsum(p)
    R = cdf[n:2 * n] - cdf[0:n]
    return cpart + a * R


def mirror_optimize(a, N, n, deadline, eta=0.05, tag="opt"):
    a = safe_normalize(a)
    if a is None:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        a /= float(n)

    best = a.copy()
    best_score = fast_score_normalized(best, N, n)
    cur_score = best_score

    it = 0
    stall = 0
    last_reset = 0
    eta_cur = float(eta)
    beta = max(1.0, float(n))
    beta_max = 200.0 * float(n)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        C = self_convolve(a, N)
        cur_score = fast_score_normalized(a, N, n)
        if not np.isfinite(cur_score):
            a = best.copy()
            break

        if cur_score < best_score:
            best_score = cur_score
            best = a.copy()

        Cmax = float(C.max())
        p = softmax_weights(C, beta)
        G = gradient_smooth(a, p, N)

        sa = float(a.sum())
        if sa <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(sa):
            a = best.copy()
            break
        gmean = float(np.dot(G, a)) / sa
        H = a * (G - gmean)

        scale = float(np.max(np.abs(H)))
        if not np.isfinite(scale) or scale <= 1e-15:
            H = _RNG.normal(0.0, 1.0, n)
            H = H - float(H.mean())
            scale = 1.0

        step = eta_cur * H / scale
        step = np.clip(step, -0.6, 0.6)
        a_new = a * np.exp(-step)
        a_new = safe_normalize(a_new)
        if a_new is None:
            a = best.copy()
            break

        score_new = fast_score_normalized(a_new, N, n)
        if not np.isfinite(score_new):
            eta_cur = max(0.001, eta_cur * 0.7)
            stall += 1
        else:
            anneal = max(0.0005, 0.015 * np.exp(-0.0005 * float(it)))
            if score_new <= cur_score + anneal:
                if score_new < cur_score:
                    eta_cur = min(0.25, eta_cur * 1.04)
                    stall = 0
                else:
                    if eta_cur > 0.012:
                        eta_cur *= 0.95
                    stall += 1
                a = a_new
                cur_score = score_new
                if cur_score < best_score:
                    best_score = cur_score
                    best = a.copy()
            else:
                eta_cur = max(0.001, eta_cur * 0.7)
                stall += 1

        beta = min(beta_max, beta * 1.001)
        it += 1

        if (cur_score > best_score + 0.035 or (stall > 700 and it - last_reset > 250)):
            improved = False
            for _ in range(12):
                amp = 0.02 + 0.18 * float(_RNG.random())
                cand = best * np.exp(_RNG.normal(0.0, amp, n))
                cand = safe_normalize(cand)
                if cand is None:
                    continue
                s2 = fast_score_normalized(cand, N, n)
                if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < cur_score:
                    a = cand
                    cur_score = s2
                    improved = True
                    if s2 < best_score:
                        best_score = s2
                        best = cand.copy()
                    break

            if not improved:
                width = max(2, int(n // 80))
                for _ in range(12):
                    cand = best.copy()
                    start = int(_RNG.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
                    stop = min(n, start + width)
                    cand[start:stop] *= float(_RNG.normal(1.0, 0.08))
                    cand[start:stop] *= np.exp(_RNG.normal(0.0, 0.12, stop - start))
                    cand = safe_normalize(cand)
                    if cand is None:
                        continue
                    s2 = fast_score_normalized(cand, N, n)
                    if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < cur_score:
                        a = cand
                        cur_score = s2
                        improved = True
                        if s2 < best_score:
                            best_score = s2
                            best = cand.copy()
                        break

            if improved:
                stall = 0
                eta_cur = 0.05

        if tag is not None and it % 800 == 0:
            print("[{}] iter={} cur={:.6f} best={:.6f} eta={:.4f} stall={}".format(
                tag, it, cur_score, best_score, eta_cur, stall))

    final_score = fast_score_normalized(best, N, n)
    if not np.isfinite(final_score):
        final_score = best_score
    return best, float(final_score)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    global _RNG
    try:
        if seed is None:
            seed = 12345
        _RNG = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    except Exception:
        _RNG = np.random.default_rng(12345)

    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 1800.0

    start = time.monotonic()
    hard_deadline = start + max(1.0, budget - 20.0)

    base = prepare_global_base()
    n, base_for_pool = choose_n_and_base(base)
    N = next_power_of_two(2 * n)

    print("search start: n={} N={}".format(n, N))

    pool = []
    top = build_candidate_pool(n, N, base_for_pool, limit=14)

    if len(top) == 0:
        raw = 1.0 / np.sqrt(np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5)
        a = safe_normalize(raw)
        if a is None:
            a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        pool.append((fast_score_normalized(a, N, n), id(a), a))
        top = [(pool[0][0], id(pool[0][2]), pool[0][2])]

    for item in top:
        pool.append(item)

    pool.sort()

    # Round 1: short-to-mid optimizations of several diverse starts.
    run_count = min(6, len(pool))
    for k in range(run_count):
        rem = hard_deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 45.0:
            break
        alloc = min(180.0, max(25.0, rem / float(run_count - k + 2)))
        a0 = pool[k][2]
        eta = 0.05 if (k % 2 == 0) else 0.025
        try:
            a1, s1 = mirror_optimize(
                a0, N, n,
                time.monotonic() + alloc,
                eta=eta,
                tag="init{}".format(k)
            )
            pool.append((s1, id(a1), a1))
            if len(pool) > 80:
                pool.sort()
                del pool[80:]
            pool.sort()
        except Exception as exc:
            print("init{} failed: {}".format(k, exc))

    pool.sort()
    if len(pool) == 0:
        raw = 1.0 / np.sqrt(np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5)
        a = safe_normalize(raw)
        if a is None:
            a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        best_a = a
        best_fast = fast_score_normalized(best_a, N, n)
    else:
        best_fast, _, best_a = pool[0]

    print("pre-final best fast score: {:.6f}".format(best_fast))

    # Round 2: long polish of the currently best object.
    if time.monotonic() < hard_deadline - 40.0:
        try:
            final_deadline = hard_deadline - 20.0
            best_a, best_fast = mirror_optimize(
                best_a, N, n,
                final_deadline,
                eta=0.03,
                tag="final"
            )
            pool.append((best_fast, id(best_a), best_a))
            pool.sort()
        except Exception as exc:
            print("final optimization failed: {}".format(exc))

    pool.sort()

    # Verify a handful of candidates with the authoritative evaluator.
    best_actual = float("inf")
    best_actual_seq = None

    # Ensure the best fast sequence is tried first.
    for item in pool:
        if time.monotonic() > hard_deadline - 2.0:
            break
        a = item[2]
        try:
            lst = [float(x) for x in a]
            actual = float(evaluate_sequence(lst))
            if np.isfinite(actual) and actual < best_actual:
                best_actual = actual
                best_actual_seq = lst
                print("actual candidate score: {:.6f}".format(actual))
            if best_actual_seq is not None and best_actual <= 1.505:
                # We already beat the stated target; stop validation early.
                break
        except Exception as exc:
            print("evaluation failure: {}".format(exc))
            continue

    if best_actual_seq is None:
        # Fallback: normalized square-root sequence.
        a = safe_normalize(1.0 / np.sqrt(np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5))
        if a is None:
            a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        best_actual_seq = [float(x) for x in a]
        try:
            best_actual = float(evaluate_sequence(best_actual_seq))
        except Exception:
            best_actual = 2.0

    print("final best actual score: {:.6f}".format(best_actual))
    return best_actual_seq
```
